Output 3: Information on climate change adaptation and its financing disseminated across the region and mainstreamed into local and national policies.

Strengthen the technical capacities of women's organizations at the local level. This represents an element of women's empowerment, facilitating access to resources, both financial and training and capacity-building opportunities for women in cooperatives and associations.

Organizing itself has an impact on their self-esteem and empowerment and generates more opportunities for women to participate in decision-making processes in cooperatives and associations also increases their participation in local development spaces.

Output 4: Financial products and services to finance sustainable investments are offered by Partner Financial Institutions (PFI), including PFI access to co-lending funds and support mechanisms.

Generate enabling conditions for women's access to financial schemes, which facilitates access to credit for women producers and women's cooperatives. To help strengthen women's entrepreneurship initiatives at the local level and with climate change adaptation and mitigation actions.

Women's economic empowerment is potentially significant at the community level, and beyond, because it increases their visibility as agents of economic growth and as policy decision-makers.

Women's cooperative groups can form the basis of collective action to address gender inequalities in a community, since women are more likely than men to be involved in activities that generate income making in the local environment. They can be an entry point to promote greater political and social mobilization for women.

In the context of the Dry Corridor, there are some fundamental limiting factors to increase access from financial institutions to women's cooperatives and associations, which are mainly related to gender roles. In this component, activities will focus on addressing some of those limiting factors to increase the access to financing schemes for female farmers. CABEI has developed and is currently implementing a methodology for the identification of Female Empowerment Mechanisms (FEMs) that can be used as a reference for the development of methodologies, tools, guidelines and processes that foster women's access to the established financial mechanisms.

5) METHODOLOGICAL CLARIFICATION

The gender assessment was developed through a desk review and a consultative process. The desk evaluation was led by a regional gender specialist with the support of seven local consultants and impacted the design of the methodology for the gender assessment, as well as the participatory assessment of common issues. A stakeholders' mapping was conducted to include the main actors and identify key women's organizations. The national consultants elaborated an overview of the selected indicators and developed a draft questionnaire based on the following dimensions: education level, life expectancy, school dropout rate, statistics of the labour situation, etc. (See Annex 2).

Furthermore, an initial socialization activity was conducted with potential end beneficiaries in October 2019, through participative workshops in each of the targeted watersheds; the details of these consultations can be found in Annex 7. A stakeholder engagement plan was defined with the gender

# Project II: Gender Action Plan Template (Project/Programme Level)

## 1) Introduction

This Gender Action Plan (GAP) forms the basis for operationalising the results and recommendations of the gender analysis section 1. It contains specific gender elements to be considered in the programme design and during the implementation of programme measures and activities. Moreover, it helps to ensure that women’s voices are heard at all levels of decision-making processes. The GAP ensures an effective gender mainstreaming and integration of a consistent gender perspective in the programme in order to address the underlying causes of vulnerability, including poverty, lack of access to resources, drivers of change and positive gender dynamics as well as to manage and mitigate potential adverse risks over the duration of the programme. The GAP ensures that the programme is compliant with GCF’s gender policy.

In striving for Ecosystem-Based Adaptation, and strengthening resilience at the community level, the regional program will take concrete measures to break down barriers to women's participation and decision-making in relation to climate change and gender relations including building on existing collaborative gender norms and practices at the household and community levels and implementing complementary strategies to improve them.

The program will contribute to gender equality through promoting exchanges among women and men. In doing so, it will address the underlying causes of vulnerability and discrimination that decrease gender forms of discrimination in terms of everyday practices, as well as those that may exist in legal and normative frameworks or other structures.

Promoting non-violent, equally-respecting relationships at the household, community and governmental levels is essential to breaking one of the most important barriers to gender and other inequalities. Effective partnerships between women and men based on more equal gender relations will also result in higher productivity, increased sources of and amount of household income, as well as other gains.

## 2) Impact Statement

The programme's objective is to increase climate-resilient sustainable development by facilitating wide-scale adoption of climate-smart agricultural technologies across the Dry Corridor of Central America and Andizones of the Dominican Republic.

The programme aims to increase and strengthen the resilience of vulnerable communities, especially women and girls, in the Dry Corridor of Central America and Andizones of the Dominican Republic to the negative impacts of climate change. This will be achieved by promoting gender equality through adaptation strategies based on Ecosystems (AEI), female heads of household; less time and work required by women for household chores; increase in time savings, women economic empowerment

## 3) Outcome Statement:


---
**Page 172**
---

Climate-resilient sustainable development by building the adaptive capacity of vulnerable communities across the Dry Corridor of Central America and the Azuero of the Dominican Republic increased.

The programme has been specifically designed to create an enabling environment that capitalizes on the scalability of EBA, focusing on four core areas of support: financial; policy; and technical. The details of these areas of support are provided below:

### Component 1: Mainstreaming of EBA, water- and resource-efficient technologies and natural resource-based businesses into selected catchments
- Contribution to the regulatory framework and policies (Technical assistance for the enhancement of regional knowledge and learning)
- Upscaling of adaptation

### Component 2: Financing and implementation for EBA as well as water- and resource-efficient technologies across selected catchments
- Creation of an enabling environment for investment in EDA, water-and-efficient technologies

#### 4) Considered outputs and activities
The program outputs and activities that will contribute to meeting these goals include:
- Equitable numbers of women as direct and indirect beneficiaries; capacity building for women and men, both preparedness and response; provision of technical assistance and financial accompaniment through extension workers; public awareness; and monitoring and reporting on formal commitments.

Through these strategies, women and men can contribute equally to making sound economic and political decisions on an equal basis, in order to achieve gender equality, women's empowerment and strengthened adaptation to climate change and sustainability.

### 5) Methodology for the elaboration of indicators and targets
The GAP includes a set of indicators and targets for each activity of the programme, as detailed below. These indicators are additional to those included in the Logical Framework. The targets were defined based on the experience of UNDP, FAO and CGIAR in the region17. Main assumptions used for their elaboration:

#### 1) The proportion of female-headed households is below 30% in all Latin-American countries where data is available:
- a. Between 20–30%; Panama, Nicaragua
- b. Between 10–20%; Dominican Republic, Costa Rica, El Salvador

#### 2) The access of female farmers to technical assistance can be between 5 and 40% lower than male farmers. This access is below 20%, in the Central American countries where data exists.

17 Please refer to sources:
- FAO 2014. Recomendaciones de políticas de agricultura familiar (pages 104, 110–113)
- FAO 2018. Las políticas públicas integran el conjunto de derechos para las mujeres

# Enabling Women’s Participation in Program Activities

A number of measures will be established by the regional program to enable women’s participation in activities. Some of them will consist of:

- Sessions held at times and locations that are safe and convenient given women's diverse activities.
- Content provided during capacity building sessions.
- Holding events in local languages or providing translation/interpretation when needed; and
- Training on gender equality, including the intersectional gender binary, in part, reflecting women’s needs, interests, priorities and potentials.

Regarding capacity building, technical assistance and extension services:

* All programme personnel, including RPMs, PEU members, PFIS, and government staff will receive training in the following topics: 1) mainstreaming gender equality in relevant technical areas; 2) promoting a participatory approach to engage women in decision-making processes against women, care economy, non-violent and equality-promoting masculinities, etc.
* The PEUs will be trained on how to design and implement projects that emphasize gender equality. The strategies will be developed using participatory methods.

Among the different products planned during the programme implementation, the RPMU and PEUs will promote the development of a Gender Equality Component or a disaggregated GAP of several of them, in accordance to the gender equality approach used in the programme. This concerns mainly, but is not limited to, the following:

* The financial mechanisms and their guidelines,
* The project management framework,
* The site-specific intervention plans for the 7 target catchments,
* The promotion of the implementation of CCA practices as well as water- and energy-efficient technologies,
* The regional knowledge hub for the dissemination of information on EBA.

Finally, given the sensitivity of GRY issues, the programme’s grievance mechanism will engage with existing local mechanisms to ensure that appropriate and confidential procedures are enacted. Safety, dignity and confidentiality of potential users, as well as a referral pathway to an UN GBV focal point for women who have experienced gender-based violence during the programme implementation through the following actions to be taken by the programme's team:

i. The PEUs will be in regular contact with local organizations, and the mapping of actors elaborated during the initial baseline phase of the programme will include which local entities can provide support to women who are victims of GBV.

ii. Regular dialogue with local authorities will also be ensured, as well as the PEU’s understanding of local gender-based violence dynamics in place that might be complementary to the multagency mechanism mentioned above.


---
**Page 174**
---

### Cumulative pressure will ensure respect of local laws and norms, and enabling actions are included in the GAP to promote positive integration on gender mainstreaming.

Persons from the affected population will have several avenues available to seek protection. Thus, dedicated hotlines, email addresses, instant messaging systems or applications, and letters and other written communication, to the extent possible, will substitute channels that require face-to-face contact and can jeopardize confidentiality.

#### 7) GAP's Budget

The programme’s GAP will be executed as all components in the design, implementation, planning, monitoring and evaluation phases. The budget for the GAP includes a Regional Gender Officer and National Advisor in gender, social participation and safeguards for the country for the 7 years of implementation. Gender enabling actions are also included in activities’ budget, as can be found in the Budget section of the Annexes (see Annex A).

An estimate budget is included in the GAP, based on the following methodology:

- **100%** of regional gender expert’s budget.
- **60%** of national advisors on gender, social participation and safeguards experts’ budget.
- **5%** of regional manager and national coordinators’ budgets
- **10%** of the total GCF Grant budget for the country, as specified in the GAP

This totals 2.6M, equivalent to 3.1% of total GCF Grant budget for this programme. This estimate will require further consideration during implementation and is provided for information purposes only.

# Activity 4.3: Technical assistance (TA) to strengthen technical capacity of CABI and network and non-regional financial institutions to access and channel

The objective under this activity will be to promote equal access to the lending and guarantee facilities for male and female farmers and other organizations through the integration of gender equality indicators in training materials for financial entities involved. Specific activities include:

a) Developing and adapting training curricula and technical assistance methodologies targeted at increasing awareness on gender equality approach.

b) Reporting and verification mechanisms developed under sub-activities 1.1.4 and 1.1.5 applied to women producer's organizations, applicants who successfully apply for loans from CABI and other lending funds from CABI in comparison with men producer's organizations that receive funding than men organizations in comparison to other lending funds from CABI.

c) At least 2 gender equality indicators are included in the monitoring, reporting, and verification mechanisms developed under sub-activities 1.1.4 and 1.1.5 including recognition of women through the RPMU and PEU gender experts

d) At least 20% increase of women producer's organizations applicants who successfully apply for loans from CABI and other lending funds in comparison to other lending funds.

e) At least 20% increase of women producer's organizations that access funding than men organizations in comparison to other lending funds.

f) At least 30% of women farmers trained through the T4T programme

g) Number of financial institutions providing gender equality training and increased their AMC capacity for women in management team

h) At least 25% of the 28 associated financial institutions providing technical assistance and increased their AMC capacity include women in their management team.

i) At least one annual campaign with gender focus for awareness on financial mechanisms

At end of programme: Estimated at: $13,948
Accredited Entity/Key cutting Agency: Gender advisor
